14:Tf9c,## Summary

When the planner narrates a coding sub-agent's `task_complete` back to the user, the **deliverable** (the printed value / command output) is frequently dropped, truncated, or buried under process chatter — while the underlying sub-agent work is correct. This is a *presentation* defect, not a capability one.

Evidence: a live 48-prompt sweep (30-scenario battery + smart/normie/harden suites) across all three backends found **0 capability-broken defects** — every answer was correct, every spawn produced its artifact, every safety probe handled. The **only** remaining defect class is this narration-envelope.

## Manifestations (all observed live)

1. **Deliverable drop on combo / printed-value tasks.** On the no-change-set completion path, `composeNarration` → `stripToolTranscript(response)` (`sub-agent-router.ts`) deletes the entire `[tool output: …] … [/tool output]` block. For a "print a value / run a command and report the output" task (no files changed → no diff), that block *is* the deliverable, so it's gone before the user sees it.
2. **Value truncation by the parent model.** The synthetic `task_complete` memory is re-rendered by the parent `RESPONSE_HANDLER` (a weak planner in some deployments), which truncates the value — e.g. `2026-06-02` → `202`, a BTC price `70234` → `705`. Per-backend signature: opencode truncates, codex emits verbose process narration + `Completed <task>` banners, claude is cleanest.
3. **Stale retry attempts leak.** When a first attempt errors (e.g. `session_state_lost` / an ACP crash) and a respawn recovers, the user sees *both* `"the sub-agent was blocked / crashed, let me try again"` **and** the eventual deliverable.
4. **Process chatter.** Sub-agents (esp. codex) narrate their steps (`"I'll load the workspace context first"`, `"rg is not installed"`, `"reverse.py already exists"`); the parent relays it.

## Proposed structural fixes

**(a) Carry the printed-value deliverable verbatim (fixes #1, mitigates #2).** A structural carrier that mirrors the existing verified-URL carrier:
- *Producer* (`sub-agent-router.ts`): on the `task_complete && !changeSet && verifiedUrls.length === 0` path, recover the captured stdout from the **raw** `response` (before `composeNarration` strips it) via the inverse of `stripToolTranscript`, and stamp it as `metadata.subAgentDeliverable` (gated to a short single block, ≤2KB, so multi-KB transcripts stay on the model-rendered path). *(I have a ready, tested producer patch for this.)*
- *Consumer* (`evaluators/sub-agent-completion.ts`): when `subAgentDeliverable` is present, relay it **verbatim** instead of letting the model re-summarize/truncate it — parallel to how `subAgentVerifiedUrls` is already consumed.

**(b) Terminal-reply filter (fixes #3, #4).** Emit only the *terminal* sub-agent reply; suppress stale `blocked`/`failed`/`state_lost` attempts that a later retry superseded, plus raw tool chatter and `Completed <task>` banners. **Caveat — needs care:** the `session_state_lost` error event is currently *coupled to the respawn trigger* (re-injecting it is what prompts the parent to respawn), so suppressing the user-facing narration requires an internal-only routing path that keeps the respawn signal. This is the part that needs design discussion.

**(c) Already shipped — source-side mitigation.** #8158 adds an output-discipline section to the scaffolded sub-agent operating manual ("lead with the deliverable, don't narrate process, no `Completed <task>` banner, a bare workspace has no SOUL.md/USER.md — don't hunt for them"), which measurably reduced #4 at the source (codex re-probes went from a verbose banner to a bare `5050` / `2026-06-02`).

## Coordination note

`sub-agent-router.ts` and `evaluators/sub-agent-completion.ts` were recently rewritten on `develop`; this should be coordinated with that work rather than landed blind. Happy to open the (a)-producer + (a)-consumer as a focused PR if the approach looks right.15:T965,## Summary
Steward login is broken in production — **both** email and Google OAuth fail with 400 — which blocks "Sign in with Eliza Cloud" for **every** app (the cli-login flow bounces all third-party apps + the dashboard through this same login).

Reproduces with plain `curl` (no browser, no extensions), so it's a server-side prod issue, not a client environment.

## Symptoms (prod, `api.elizacloud.ai`)
**Google/OAuth** — `GET /steward/auth/oauth/google/authorize?...&response_type=code`:
```
{"ok":false,"error":"code_challenge is required for response_type=code"}
```
Adding `code_challenge` + `code_challenge_method=S256` still returns **400**.

**Email** — `POST /steward/auth/email/send` → **400 Bad Request** (from `steward-login-section.tsx` → `signInWithEmail`).

## Root cause (the OAuth half)
The Steward `/authorize` endpoint now **requires PKCE** (`code_challenge`), but:
- `packages/cloud-frontend/src/pages/login/steward-oauth-url.ts` → `buildStewardOAuthAuthorizeUrl()` builds the URL with `response_type: "code"` and **no** `code_challenge` / `code_challenge_method`.
- The exchange flow is **nonce-based with no verifier**: `packages/cloud-api/auth/steward-nonce-exchange/route.ts` documents *"The Steward API authenticates the exchange purely by possession of the one-time `code` — there is no client [verifier]."*

So the authorize service (PKCE-required) and the cloud login flow (nonce-based, no PKCE) are out of sync — and even a hand-added `code_challenge` still 400s, suggesting a deeper Steward auth misconfig/regression in prod.

## Impact
No one can sign in via Eliza Cloud right now — dashboard, edad, and every third-party app using the cli-login "Sign in with Eliza Cloud" bounce here and dead-end at this login.

## Fix options
- **A (restore parity):** make Steward `/authorize` accept the nonce flow **without** `code_challenge` (matches the existing nonce-exchange contract). Simplest if PKCE was enabled unintentionally.
- **B (full PKCE):** frontend generates a `code_verifier` + S256 `code_challenge`, sends `code_challenge` at `/authorize` and stores the verifier; `steward-nonce-exchange` forwards `code_verifier` to `/auth/oauth/exchange`.

The `email/send` 400 handler isn't in this monorepo (Steward service) — flag for that team too; it may share the same prod regression.

Found via the cli-login flow from a third-party browser app.
16:T635,## Summary

`packages/app-core`'s built `dist/entry.js` imports transitive runtime deps (`jose`, `@node-rs/argon2`, and likely more) as **external** packages. These deps live in Bun's content-addressable store but are not linked at the top level where Node-style resolution looks, so any command that runs `dist/entry.js` outside Bun's workspace-aware loader fails with `Cannot find module`.

Surfaced by a downstream QA smoke pass (milady-ai/milady#2179, finding #1 "layer 2").

## Repro

1. Build the app-core entry (`dist/entry.js`, ~7.6 MB across ~20 chunks).
2. Run it with a plain Node-style loader (e.g. via `doctor`, or any path that doesn't go through Bun's workspace loader).
3. First failure: `Cannot find module 'jose'`. Manually linking `jose` from Bun's store unblocks it, then it immediately hits `Cannot find module '@node-rs/argon2'`, and so on — whack-a-mole suggesting dozens more.

## Root cause

The bundler (tsdown) marks these runtime deps `external`, but they are transitive (not hoisted to the top-level `node_modules`), so Node-style resolution from `dist/entry.js` can't find them.

## Proposed fixes (pick one)

- (a) Bundle the needed transitive runtime deps **inline** in `dist/entry.js` (tsdown config: move them out of `external`).
- (b) Declare all needed runtime deps as **direct deps** of the package so they hoist to the top level.
- (c) Ship a runtime/loader that knows how to resolve into Bun's store.

## Severity

Medium — affects any command that runs `dist/entry.js` outside Bun's loader (e.g. `doctor`), and is broader than a single command.
17:Tfed,## Summary

Two release-orchestration workflows hit `startup_failure` on every tag push (most recently on `v2.0.3`):

- [Release All Platforms](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/actions/runs/26156834072) — `.github/workflows/release-all.yml`, triggered by `push: tags: [v*.*.*]`
- [elizaOS Full OS Release](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/actions/runs/26156833438) — `.github/workflows/elizaos-os-full-release.yml`, triggered by `release: types: [created]`

Both fail before any job runs (`This run likely failed because of a workflow file issue.`). YAML parses cleanly locally; all referenced reusable workflows exist at the tag.

## Root cause

Both orchestrators call reusable workflows whose `secrets:` blocks declare `required: true` for repo secrets that don't exist in `elizaOS/eliza`. When `secrets: inherit` can't supply a `required: true` secret, GitHub fails the parent at startup.

Confirmed via `gh secret list -R elizaOS/eliza` that these are missing:

| Secret | Required by | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| `DEBIAN_GPG_PRIVATE_KEY` | `publish-apt-repo.yml` | sign apt repo |
| `DEBIAN_GPG_KEY_ID` | `publish-apt-repo.yml` | sign apt repo |
| `DEBIAN_GPG_PASSPHRASE` | `publish-apt-repo.yml` | sign apt repo |
| `CSC_LINK` | `release-electrobun.yml` (macOS sign) | Developer ID .p12 (base64) |
| `CSC_KEY_PASSWORD` | `release-electrobun.yml` | .p12 password |
| `APPLE_ID` | `release-electrobun.yml`, `apple-store-release.yml` | notarization Apple ID |
| `APPLE_APP_SPECIFIC_PASSWORD` | `release-electrobun.yml`, `apple-store-release.yml` | notarization |
| `APPLE_TEAM_ID` | `release-electrobun.yml`, `apple-store-release.yml` | Apple Developer Team ID |
| `ITC_TEAM_ID` | `apple-store-release.yml` | App Store Connect team ID |
| `APP_STORE_APP_ID` | `apple-store-release.yml` | numeric app ID |
| `MATCH_PASSWORD` | `apple-store-release.yml` | Fastlane Match passphrase |
| `MATCH_GIT_URL` | `apple-store-release.yml` | Fastlane Match repo |
| `WINDOWS_SIGN_CERT_BASE64` | `windows-store-release.yml` | Windows code-signing cert |
| `WINDOWS_SIGN_CERT_PASSWORD` | `windows-store-release.yml` | cert password |
| `WINDOWS_SIGN_TIMESTAMP_URL` | `windows-store-release.yml` | RFC 3161 timestamp server |
| `FLATHUB_TOKEN` | `flatpak-publish.yml` | declared `required: false`, not blocking |

The full secret list expected is documented in the header comment of `.github/workflows/release-all.yml`.

## Why the other release workflows worked

`NPM Release`, `Build Agent Image`, `Build elizaOS Linux ISO`, `Build elizaOS Debian Package` (build-only, not publish), `Release AOSP Flasher`, `Release Orchestrator`, `Snap Build & Test`, `Build & Release (Electrobun)`, `Release USB Installer` all started fine — their workflows don't `require: true` any missing secret. (USB Installer separately failed mid-run on a now-fixed `electrobun` PATH issue.)

## Resolution options

1. **Add the missing secrets** to `elizaOS/eliza` → Settings → Secrets and variables → Actions. This is the intended end state per the workflow header docs.
2. **Downgrade `required: true` → `required: false`** in the reusable workflows for platforms we don't intend to ship yet (e.g. if Windows Store / Apple Store / signed apt repo aren't in scope for v2.0.x). The leaf workflows already self-guard most calls.
3. **Stop calling unconfigured publish workflows** from `release-all.yml` until their secrets exist.

Probably want a mix: add Debian GPG (lowest-risk, internal-only) and Apple signing (highest-value), and gate Windows Store + Flathub behind explicit opt-in until those credentials are arranged.

## Context

- Tag for `v2.0.3` is live on `main` (`fa240156ed`). All other release workflows (NPM, Linux ISO, Android, Snap, Electrobun, etc.) ran or are running normally.
- Failed runs are also reproducible on the prior `v2.0.2` attempt — this is the pre-existing block, not a new regression.
- I don't have admin rights to download the run logs, which would confirm the exact secret-validation error message. Anyone with admin can drop the snippet here for the record.18:T776,## context

after #7988 lands the windows-x86_64 ffi build path, `libeliza-llama-shim.dll` loads into `bun:ffi` with all 39 symbols resolved. the very next layer — prewarm — crashes with a ggml batch-sizing assert on `eliza-1-0_8b` text bundle.

## crash

```
GGML_ASSERT(n_tokens_all <= cparams.n_batch) failed
```

repro: `~/.eliza` already has the `eliza-1-0_8b-128k.gguf` bundle staged; on first activation the prewarm batch builder asks the runtime to decode more tokens than the context's `n_batch` slot allows. fatal.

## environment

- platform: windows-x86_64
- backend: cuda (cuda 12.4, msvc, ninja, llama.cpp pinned to the ref in `build-llama-cpp-desktop-dylib.mjs`)
- shim: built from `eliza_llama_shim.c` with the stub set added in #7988
- bundle: `bundles/0_8b/text/eliza-1-0_8b-128k.gguf` from default-user `~/.eliza/local-inference/`
- runtime: `desktop-llama-adapter.ts` → `eliza_llama_decode_unified` path

## what i think is going on

the prewarm path appears to construct a batch that targets the manifest's declared context window (128k tokens) without first clamping against the actual `cparams.n_batch` value the ffi context was created with. on linux/macos this might be masked by larger default `n_batch` or different prewarm sizing; on windows-cuda the assert fires.

candidates (i don't want to put words in anyone's mouth):

1. prewarm should size the batch off `cparams.n_batch`, not `n_ctx`.
2. context creation on the desktop adapter should set `n_batch >= prewarm_tokens` for the chosen bundle.
3. the bundle manifest's recommended prewarm token count should be exposed and used as both inputs.

## scope

- not blocked by anything outstanding once #7988 lands.
- separate from the build-time fixes in #7988 (which are infrastructure) — this is a runtime sizing bug one layer down.
- related context PR: #7983 (local-inference dflash discovery + runtime router wiring).
19:T96c,## Summary

When a sub-agent invokes a skill via USE_SKILL (e.g. weather skill running \`wttr.in\`), the bot's reply to Discord contains the raw bash command + JSON wrapping AS prose, then the clean output, glued together:

\`\`\`
{"cmd":["bash","-lc","curl -s \"wttr.in/Paris?format=%l:+%c+%t+%h+%w\""]}{"output":"Paris: 🌦️ +15°C 68% ↙3km/h"}Paris: 🌦️ +15°C 68% ↙3 km/h
\`\`\`

Three layers leaked verbatim:
1. \`{"cmd":["bash","-lc",...]}\` — the bash invocation envelope
2. \`{"output":"..."}\` — the captured-output envelope
3. The actual user-friendly result (also rendered, fortunately)

## Repro

Live 2026-05-25 07:49 UTC. Probe: \`@bot use the weather skill for paris\`. Trajectory \`tj-1be9e601583b7e.json\`. Stage 0: \`simple=true reqTool=false candidates=["WEATHER_FETCH"]\` reply="On it." (routing-layer promoted to planning via the candidateActions->planning fix). Iter 1: \`USE_SKILL slug=weather mode=auto\`. The skill captured stdout via a wrapper that emits the cmd+output envelopes; the planner's REPLY text concatenated all three.

Probe \`use the weather skill to get weather for tokyo\` had the same shape but the wrapping was less visible because the final paraphrase was cleaner.

## Why it matters

User-facing UX: the bot dumps internal shell invocation details to Discord. Looks broken; reveals "I am running bash". Pollutes the channel.

## Suggested fix

USE_SKILL action's result construction should produce a clean \`userFacingText\` (the actual command output) separate from the diagnostic \`text\` (which can include cmd + envelopes for trajectory recording). The planner-loop's \`preferredFinalMessageFromToolOrModel\` already prefers \`userFacingText\` when set with \`verifiedUserFacing: true\` — USE_SKILL should opt in for skills whose stdout is the user answer (weather, github, healthcheck, etc.).

Or simpler interim fix: in the skill-invocation wrapper, when producing the REPLY text, parse + strip the \`{"cmd":...}\` and \`{"output":...}\` envelopes before they're concatenated into the model's REPLY context.

## Acceptance

- \`use the weather skill for paris\` returns just \`Paris: 🌦️ +15°C 68% ↙3 km/h\`, no JSON envelopes.
- No regression on skills that already produce clean output (some did in this same sweep — github, healthcheck honest refusals didn't leak).

Reported via 50-probe deep sweep on milady deployment 2026-05-25.1a:T839,## Summary

The `/orchestrator` workbench (`@elizaos/plugin-task-coordinator`) conversation surface has several UI-parity gaps versus the design target (Codex/Claude-style coding-agent UIs), plus one backend prose-assembly bug. This issue tracks bringing the conversation view to a clean, premium, conversation-centric parity bar.

## Problems

**Conversation surface (`plugin-task-coordinator`)**
- The workbench inherits a blue tint: `base.css` routes `--bg-accent` / `--ring` / `--info` / `--link` through `--brand-blue`, so the room reads cold-blue (design rule is no blue).
- Tool-call cards are over-chromed: the shell command is echoed twice (header + a `$`-bar), a wall-clock timestamp is stamped on every row (5+ identical stamps per viewport), and cards print the raw tool name (`bash`) instead of an action verb.
- Output is truncated head-only, dropping the meaningful tail (errors/exit summary live at the end).
- The primary CTA is a loud full-saturation orange fill; loading states pop in with bare centered text (no skeleton).
- No collapsible "thinking" cell for reasoning.

**Backend (`plugin-agent-orchestrator`)**
- Assistant prose loses word-boundary spaces (`"is"+"proven" -> "isproven"`): `extractAssistantText()` joins a codex-acp word-split content-block array with `join("")`.
- `agent_thought_chunk` (reasoning) is silently dropped in the `session/update` handler — only token counts are captured, so reasoning text never reaches the UI.

**Dev experience**
- `node --watch` re-evaluates the whole `eliza-source` module graph, so unrelated edits reboot the agent (and reopen the PGlite vault).

## Proposed

A focused conversation-UI parity pass: de-blue (scoped token override), real interleaved diff, slim cards with status-aware verb labels + exit/duration meta, markdown via the `marked` token AST, two-tier turn rhythm, head+tail truncation, de-loud CTA + skeletons + streaming cue, a collapsible reasoning cell; the backend prose re-spacing + reasoning forwarding; and scoping `--watch` to source roots. Verified with the existing unit suite + a new live Playwright e2e.1b:T688,### Summary

`verifyLocalTarget` in `plugins/plugin-agent-orchestrator/src/services/sub-agent-router.ts` false-positives on a healthy, served static app, marking it "dead" purely because its local file mtime is older than `session.createdAt - 5s`.

### Root cause

In the completion-URL verification pass (`annotateUnverifiedUrls`), after a URL probes a healthy HTTP 200 (`probe()` returns `status === null`), the code still consults `verifyMappedLocalUrl` → `verifyLocalTarget`, which applies a wall-clock freshness gate:

```ts
if (requireFresh && stat.mtimeMs < sessionStartedAtMs - 5000) {
  return `mapped local target was not updated during this session: ...`;
}
```

A static app (e.g. `color-pop/index.html`) that exists, is non-empty, and serves HTTP 200 gets flagged "dead" because the deploy step copied the build into place and preserved the source file's mtime (mtime skew vs `session.createdAt`).

### Impact (GAP-C chain)

1. The false-dead triggers a spurious verify-retry.
2. The retry suppresses the round-1 `task_complete`.
3. The in-flight retry session trips `newerTaskSince` in `coding-session-changes.ts`.
4. The REAL diff is withheld — so a follow-up "what did you change?" wrongly answers "no captured file diff".

### Fix

The live HTTP 200 probe is authoritative for a served URL. The mtime-freshness comparison must not override a healthy 200; reserve the freshness gate for cases where the URL is NOT serving (where the local file is the only liveness signal). The missing-file and empty-file checks remain unconditional.

Tracked by PR with a regression test that pins both the served-200 (not flagged) and unserved (still flagged) cases.1c:T83d,## Summary

PR #8097 added two things:
1. A **keyless `WEB_FETCH` action** (`packages/agent/...`) so non-Anthropic models can answer live info inline. **This is fine — keep it.**
2. **Inline WEB_FETCH planner routing** in `packages/core/src/services/message.ts`. **This regresses live-info on non-Anthropic runtimes and should be reverted.**

## The regression

On a gpt-oss / Cerebras (non-Anthropic) runtime, the inline routing added to `message.ts` breaks live-info queries (BTC price, weather, etc.):

- It **perturbs the working opencode sub-agent webfetch path** that previously answered these queries reliably.
- `WEB_FETCH` **never reliably surfaces** through the planner action surface on these runtimes, so the planner is left mis-routing — live-info queries stop returning real values.

Live-testing confirmed that reverting `message.ts` to its pre-#8097 state (`git checkout <pre-8097> -- packages/core/src/services/message.ts`) plus a stronger spawn-task prompt restores correct behavior: BTC/weather queries again return real values via the opencode webfetch tool.

## The inline-routing additions to revert (all in `message.ts`)

- the live-info **force-include** block in `collectV5PlannerCandidateActions` that surfaces `WEB_FETCH` past the context gate;
- the **`webLookupBackstop`** branch in `buildV5PlannerActionSurface` (+ the `V5PlannerActionSurfaceSummary.webLookupBackstop` field);
- the `WEB_LOOKUP_ACTION_NAMES` / `WEB_LOOKUP_DIRECT_ACTIONS` constants, `findCanonicalWebLookupActionName`, and `findPreferredWebLookupActionName`;
- the `"web"` branch added to `shouldPreferDirectCurrentCandidateActions`;
- the web/coding reordering + `findPreferredWebLookupActionName` swap in `inferAckIntentCandidateActions` / `inferDirectCurrentRequestCandidateActions` / `findDirectOwnedActionSuggestion`.

## What stays

The keyless **`WEB_FETCH` action** in `packages/agent/...` is harmless and stays — capable models can still use it. Only the `message.ts` planner *routing* is reverted, so upstream matches the working live state and future syncs don't re-break live-info.

Happy to open a docs PR to fix the broken link once the correct destination is confirmed.1f:T8ea,## Summary

`develop` CI is currently red on **Server Tests** / **Plugin Tests** / **Zero-Key E2E** / **Build production Docker image** because vite-based tests fail to resolve the new `@elizaos/logger` package:

```
Error: Failed to resolve entry for package "@elizaos/logger".
The package may have incorrect main/module/exports specified in its package.json.
 ❯ packageEntryFailure node_modules/.bun/vite@.../vite/dist/node/chunks/config.js
FAIL packages/agent/src/__tests__/views-system-smoke.test.ts
FAIL packages/agent/src/__tests__/views-registry-integration.test.ts
FAIL packages/agent/src/__tests__/plugin-tui-view-coverage.test.ts
```

(This blocks every open PR against `develop`, since they all inherit it.)

## Root cause

`packages/logger/package.json` resolves `.` to **`./dist/index.js`** by default, with a non-default `eliza-source` condition pointing at `./src/index.ts`:

```jsonc
"exports": {
  ".": {
    "types": "./dist/index.d.ts",
    "eliza-source": { "import": "./src/index.ts", "default": "./src/index.ts" },
    "import": "./dist/index.js",
    "default": "./dist/index.js"
  }
}
```

`@elizaos/core` depends on it (`"@elizaos/logger": "workspace:*"`, imported in `packages/core/src/logger.ts`). Local/dev setups resolve fine because they run with `--conditions eliza-source` (→ `src`). But the CI vite test runner uses the **`default`** condition → `./dist/index.js`, and **`packages/logger/dist` is not built** before these tests run — so resolution fails.

The test build steps (e.g. Plugin Tests' `run build --filter=@elizaos/core …`) don't build `@elizaos/logger`, and it doesn't get built as a transitive dep in that path either.

## Suggested fixes (pick one)

1. **Build `@elizaos/logger` before the agent/server vite tests** — add it to the build filter list in the test scripts (or ensure turbo builds it as a `^build` dep of `@elizaos/core`).
2. **Resolve `@elizaos/logger` to `src` in the vite test config** — set `resolve.conditions: ['eliza-source', …]` in the agent test vite/vitest config, matching how the runtime resolves it.

Happy to PR whichever direction maintainers prefer.

## Evidence

- `page.goto(route, { waitUntil: "domcontentloaded", timeout: 60_000 })` exceeds 60s for the two routes only.

- The audit log shows `[vite] (client) [optimizer] scanning dependencies... bundling dependencies...` during the first route load — the dev dep-optimizer fires on first navigation (alphabetically `landing`/`/` is first) and its bundling step delays `DOMContentLoaded` past 60s under headless Chromium.

22:T1c4e,## Summary

Native ElizaCloud agents are currently inconsistent after the Hetzner/control-plane cutover:

- A newly-created native agent can be listed, but provisioning fails after retries. One retry gets to `Sandbox health check timed out`; the final job error is `ELIZA_KMS_BACKEND=steward requires steward.{baseUrl, tokenProvider}`.
- An existing agent is marked `running` and the container log job proves the runtime is alive, but both the Cloud bridge endpoint and the public subdomain are unreachable.
- This does not look like a client app integration issue. The app-scoped Cloud API remains healthy; the failure boundary is native agent provisioning/routing.

Credentials, tenant identifiers, agent IDs, job IDs, host IPs, node names, and app-specific prompt/config content are intentionally redacted from this public issue. I can share exact IDs privately with maintainers if needed.

## Live evidence, redacted

Source baseline checked locally: current `origin/develop` at the time of testing.

### Existing running agent is alive over logs but unreachable over bridge/public ingress

`GET /api/compat/agents/:id/status` returns a running agent record similar to:

```json
{
  "status": "running",
  "lastHeartbeat": "recent timestamp",
  "bridgeUrl": "http://<docker-host>:<dynamic-bridge-port>",
  "webUiUrl": "https://<agent-id>.waifu.fun",
  "currentNode": "<node-id>",
  "databaseStatus": "ready"
}
```

But `POST /api/v1/eliza/agents/:id/bridge` with a JSON-RPC heartbeat returns:

```json
{
  "jsonrpc": "2.0",
  "id": "probe-heartbeat",
  "error": {
    "code": -32000,
    "message": "Sandbox bridge is unreachable"
  }
}
```

Direct probes to the saved bridge URL time out:

- `http://<docker-host>:<dynamic-bridge-port>/` -> timeout
- `http://<docker-host>:<dynamic-bridge-port>/api/health` -> timeout
- `http://<docker-host>:<dynamic-bridge-port>/health` -> timeout

Public ingress also fails:

- `https://<agent-id>.waifu.fun/` -> Cloudflare `502`
- `https://<agent-id>.waifu.fun/api/agents` -> Cloudflare `502`

However, `GET /api/compat/agents/:id/logs` completes and shows the container runtime is up:

```text
[eliza-api] Listening on http://0.0.0.0:2138
[eliza] Control UI: http://localhost:2138
[eliza] Running in server-only mode (no interactive chat)
[eliza-api] Serving dashboard UI from /app/packages/app/dist
```

That suggests the control-plane/log path can reach the node/container, but the saved bridge/public routes cannot reach the published runtime.

### New agent provisioning shows mixed worker/config failures

For a fresh native agent provision job, polling first reported:

```json
{
  "status": "pending",
  "result": {
    "error": "Sandbox health check timed out",
    "status": "error",
    "cloudAgentId": "<agent-id>"
  },
  "error": "Sandbox health check timed out",
  "attempts": 1,
  "maxAttempts": 3
}
```

The final state became:

```json
{
  "status": "failed",
  "result": {
    "error": "Sandbox health check timed out",
    "status": "error",
    "cloudAgentId": "<agent-id>"
  },
  "error": "ELIZA_KMS_BACKEND=steward requires steward.{baseUrl, tokenProvider}",
  "attempts": 3,
  "maxAttempts": 3
}
```

The agent record then has:

```json
{
  "status": "error",
  "databaseStatus": "ready",
  "bridgeUrl": null,
  "errorMessage": "Provisioning permanently failed after 3 attempts: ELIZA_KMS_BACKEND=steward requires steward.{baseUrl, tokenProvider}"
}
```

This looks like multiple provisioning workers or retries with inconsistent runtime env/config: one attempt reaches Docker health checking, another dies during managed API-key/KMS work.

### Route/API sanity checks

The mounted native routes are agent-based, not instances-based:

- `GET /api/v1/eliza/agents` -> 200
- `GET /api/compat/agents` -> 200
- `GET /api/v1/instances` -> 404
- `GET /api/v1/eliza/instances` -> 404
- `GET /api/instances` -> 404

`GET /api/health` returns 200, but `GET /api/health/operational` returns `degraded` with a `steward_platform.configured: false` check.

## Source observations

Relevant current code paths:

- `packages/cloud-shared/src/lib/services/managed-eliza-config.ts` creates a managed per-agent API key during environment preparation (`prepareManagedElizaBaseEnvironment`). That is the KMS-touching path before Docker launch.
- `packages/security/src/kms/index.ts` defaults non-local/non-test KMS to `steward`; it only falls back to local if `ELIZA_LOCAL_ROOT_KEY` is present. Otherwise it throws `ELIZA_KMS_BACKEND=steward requires steward.{baseUrl, tokenProvider}`.
- `packages/cloud-shared/src/lib/services/docker-sandbox-provider.ts` sets `ELIZA_PORT` and `PORT` to the agent port, sets `BRIDGE_PORT`, and publishes both allocated host ports to the same container `PORT`:
  - `-p ${bridgePort}:${PORT}`
  - `-p ${webUiPort}:${PORT}`
- `docker-sandbox-provider.ts` health checks `http://127.0.0.1:${bridgePort}/api/health` and `http://127.0.0.1:${webUiPort}/` from the Docker host.
- `packages/scripts/cloud/admin/daemons/agent-router.ts` resolves public subdomain routing to `${host}:${webUiPort}`, preferring `headscale_ip` and otherwise falling back to the bridge URL host.

Given the live symptoms, the likely boundaries are:

1. At least one production provisioning worker is missing valid KMS config (`ELIZA_KMS_BACKEND=local` + persistent `ELIZA_LOCAL_ROOT_KEY`, or a real Steward KMS config/token provider).
2. The Docker host dynamic ports / agent-router / firewall path is not consistently reachable from public ingress or Cloud API bridge calls. Affected records observed during testing had no `headscale_ip`, so the current route depends on public host + dynamic ports being reachable.

## Expected behavior

- A provisioning worker with invalid KMS config should report unhealthy and avoid claiming jobs, instead of consuming retries.
- A native agent should not be left as `running` if Cloud's bridge endpoint and public subdomain cannot reach it.
- A successful provision should produce a reachable bridge/public route, or fail with one clear actionable provisioning error.

## Suggested next checks / fixes

- Verify all production provisioning-worker instances use the same env and no stale workers are still claiming jobs.
- Explicitly configure KMS on the provisioning worker host(s): either `ELIZA_KMS_BACKEND=local` with a persistent `ELIZA_LOCAL_ROOT_KEY`, or wire real Steward KMS `baseUrl`/`tokenProvider` into `createKmsClient` before using `ELIZA_KMS_BACKEND=steward`.
- Verify Hetzner firewall/security groups/Nginx allow the dynamic port ranges used by Docker sandboxes (`BRIDGE_PORT_MIN..MAX`, `WEBUI_PORT_MIN..MAX`) from the routing layer, or switch the router/bridge path to a stable internal route.
- If Headscale is intended to be the route source, ensure containers register and persist `headscale_ip`.
- Surface the health timeout diagnostics already collected by `docker-sandbox-provider.ts` into the job result or an admin-visible field, so operators do not need sidecar logs to see `docker port`, `docker inspect`, and container logs.
- Consider adding a provisioning-worker startup preflight for KMS, Docker node reachability, and routing config. If any required dependency is missing, fail the worker health check instead of claiming jobs.
23:T730,## Context

#8046 fixed the dogsite mis-route where a build request ("update the website, add some fixes") fell through to plugin-lifeops `SCHEDULED_TASKS` (via its `TASKS` simile), which errored with "I need a trigger." The fix steers build intents to the coding parent at two layers:
- the planner prompt's domain-routing rule (build/create/update/fix a website/app → code), and
- `looksLikeTaskAgentRequest` surfacing the coding `TASKS` parent in the action catalog.

## The residual gap

`scheduled-task.ts`'s `validate()` is permission-only (`hasLifeOpsAccess` + `roleGate`). There is **no intent-layer backstop**: if the planner ever names a scheduler action for a hands-on build request *despite* the prompt + catalog routing, nothing structurally rejects it.

## Why I'm filing this as a question, not a PR

An earlier attempt added an inline negative guard to the scheduler path; it was dropped as redundant/regressive against the routing changes above. I think that was the right call:
- A regex "does this look like build work?" check inside `validate()` is the regex-on-LLM-intent pattern this project deliberately avoids, and
- `validate()` is about action *availability/permission*, not intent routing — putting routing logic there is a layering smell.

So my recommendation is that the prompt + catalog layer from #8046 is likely sufficient and **no backstop is needed**. Filing this only to close the loop on the audit finding rather than drop it silently.

## If a backstop IS wanted

Do it structurally, not with a regex in `validate()` — e.g. via the shared routing taxonomy that already classifies build vs. lifeops intent, applied once at the candidate-selection layer, so the scheduler and the prompt agree by construction.

Refs #8046, #8047. Feel free to close if the prompt/catalog layer is considered enough.24:Ta4a,## Summary
App-credits purchases and inference spend use **two different ledgers**, so credits a user buys for a monetized app can't be spent — and a user with cloud/org credits is blocked from a monetized app unless they separately top up the app pool. This blocks third-party browser apps monetizing via `X-App-Id` end-to-end.

This looks like a half-finished migration (see the `// Switched from app_credit_balances ... to the org balance` comment), so flagging rather than unilaterally changing billing.

## Details (origin/develop)
- **Purchase funds the per-app pool.** `processPurchase` credits `app_credit_balances` via `appCreditBalancesRepository.addCredits(...)` — `packages/cloud-shared/src/lib/services/app-credits.ts:~307`.
- **Balance check reads the per-app pool.** `checkBalance` → `appCreditBalancesRepository.findByAppAndUser(...)` — `app-credits.ts:~193`.
- **Spend debits the ORG balance.** The spend path was switched to `creditsService.reserveAndDeductCredits({ organizationId })` — `app-credits.ts:~408` (the "Switched from app_credit_balances ... to the org balance" comment). The `X-App-Id` completions path also reserves against the org: `reserveCredits({ organizationId })` — `packages/cloud-api/v1/chat/completions/route.ts:~934`.

## Impact
1. A user buys app-credits → funds land in `app_credit_balances` → inference debits the **org** balance → "Insufficient credits" against an empty org balance. **Money paid, credits never spendable.**
2. `checkBalance` gates on the app pool, so a user who *does* have org/cloud credits is rejected from a monetized app unless they top up the app pool too.
3. The `/api/v1/apps/[id]/chat` 402 says "Insufficient app credits" but deducts from the org balance (`route.ts:~295-333`), so the error misdirects users.

## Suggested fix (pick one ledger)
- **A — org-only (matches the "Switched" comment's intent):** make `processPurchase` credit the **org** balance (and have `/api/v1/app-credits/balance` + `checkBalance` report/read org). App-credits checkout becomes "top up cloud credits for this app"; creator still earns markup via `recordCreatorEarnings()`. Simplest if the per-app pool is being retired.
- **B — app-pool-only:** make the `X-App-Id` spend path read+debit `app_credit_balances` instead of the org balance.

Happy to open the PR once you confirm the direction (A or B) — A looks consistent with the existing comment + `recordCreatorEarnings`. Also a one-line follow-up: word the `/apps/[id]/chat` 402 to match whichever ledger is checked.

Found while building a third-party app (SupaKan) on the `X-App-Id` app-credits flow.
25:T945,## Summary

In `plugin-local-inference`'s voice pipeline, the plumbing to tie a recognized voice to an elizaOS `Entity` is fully present, but the **bind step appears to be unreachable at runtime** — `VoiceProfileStore.bindEntity` / `unbindEntity` have no caller outside tests. As a result a profile's `entityId` stays `null` in practice: attribution can resolve *which voice* is speaking, but the agent never persists *who that is* into the relationship graph.

> Grep-level finding on current `develop` — happy to be corrected if there's a dynamic/registered path I missed.

## What already works (so this isn't a rebuild)

- Voice profiles carry and persist an `entityId` — `plugins/plugin-local-inference/src/services/voice/profile-store.ts` (`bindEntity` L605, `unbindEntity` L627).
- Attribution resolves the entity on recognition — `src/services/voice/speaker/attribution-pipeline.ts:199` (`entityId: refined?.entityId ?? match.profile.entityId ?? null`).
- The UI already crowns the OWNER when the speaking voice's entity matches the owner — `packages/ui/.../ChatVoiceStatusBar` (`speakerEntityId === ownerEntityId`).

## The gap

`grep -rn bindEntity` across `plugins/` + `packages/` returns only the definitions in `profile-store.ts` and references in `__tests__/voice-profile-store.test.ts`. The voice-profile routes (`src/services/voice/voice-profile-routes.ts`) expose `activate` / list / soft-delete, but **no bind/claim endpoint**, and there is no agent action that binds. So there is no runtime path (route, action, or UI affordance) for "this voice = this entity."

Consequence: `entityId` stays `null`, the OWNER-crown only lights up in tests, and recognized speaker identity never reaches the relationship graph — the agent hears a known voice but can't remember *who* across sessions.

## Suggested shape (open to direction)

1. A bind/unbind route alongside the existing voice-profile routes, e.g. `POST /v1/voice/profiles/:id/bind { entityId }`.
2. An agent action so the agent can bind by voice ("this is &lt;name&gt;"), mirroring how `GENERATE_MEDIA` is wired.
3. On bind, route through `EntityStore` / `RelationshipStore` + the identity merge engine (per the root `CLAUDE.md` rule — don't bypass it), so the bound voice becomes a first-class relationship node.

Happy to take a first cut if this is wanted and not already in progress.

## Proposed design (mirrors the existing `quickContext` pattern)

cc @shawmakesmagic — which option (A/B) + replace-vs-augment do you want? Then I'll ship the read-only slice.28:T870,## Summary

When the planner runs a SHELL/USE_SKILL/etc tool and then emits a REPLY in a follow-up iteration, the REPLY text occasionally summarizes that the result was provided rather than including the actual data. The user sees a meta-statement and no answer.

## Repro

Probes on 2026-05-25:
- **Prompt**: "list the names of files in /etc that have 'hostname' in them — actually run it"
- **Trajectory**: \`tj-c608892af9b5d3.json\`. Stage 0: \`simple=false, requiresTool=true, candidates=["BASH"]\`. Iter 1: SHELL \`grep -rl "hostname" /etc 2>/dev/null\` (real output). Iter 2: not shown; the bot's REPLY was:
- **Reply**: \`"Listed files from grep output; provided concise list as requested."\` — no actual filenames

Same pattern observed at \`tj-dc46d718c97add.json\` on retest:
- Reply: \`"Provided the list of /etc files that contain the word 'hostname' as returned by grep."\` — again, no list.

Other probes (df -h, uname -a, wc -l /etc/passwd, curl coingecko, etc.) included the tool's actual output verbatim — the bug is intermittent.

## Root cause hypothesis

The model in iter 2 (REPLY iteration) is reading the prior tool result, producing a textual summary of the act of returning the data, and skipping the data itself. May be related to specific prompt wordings ("list the names of files", "actually run it") that the model interprets as "describe what you did" rather than "include the output".

## Suggested approach

Two routes:
1. **Tool opt-in for \`verifiedUserFacing\`**: when SHELL produces a clean stdout, set \`verifiedUserFacing=true\` so \`preferredFinalMessageFromToolOrModel\` prefers the tool's text over the model's REPLY paraphrase. Currently SHELL doesn't opt in.
2. **Planner-prompt rule**: explicitly forbid meta-narration ("Listed/Provided/Returned X as requested") when the REPLY follows a tool that produced stdout.

(1) is preferred — structural, no regex on output.

## Acceptance

- "list files in /etc with hostname" reliably returns the grep output, not the summary.
- No regression on probes that already work (df -h, wc -l, uname -a, etc).

Reported via live deepscan on milady deployment 2026-05-25.29:Tc7e,## Summary

In the `packages/ui` Storybook catalog, stories for **feature-surface components that transitively import `@elizaos/core`** cannot render in the browser preview. This tracks the root cause + fix options so the deferred stories can be added once the harness lands.

Scope: Storybook-harness follow-up for the work on branch `nubs/continuous-chat-overlay` (commits `0fceb71f9c`, `f4abd24189`). The purely-presentational stories already ship and render; this is only about the i18n / model-hub surfaces. Full write-up lives in `packages/ui/.storybook/README.md` (limitation #3).

## Affected stories (written, held back — not committed broken)

- `apps/RunningAppsRow`, `apps/AppIdentity`
- `local-inference/{ActiveModelBar, DownloadProgress, ModelUpdatesPanel}`
- `policy-controls/{PolicyToggle, RateLimitSection, SpendingLimitSection, AutoApproveSection, TimeWindowSection, ApprovedAddressesSection}`

## Root cause

These components reach `@elizaos/core` via `state/TranslationContext` → `state/persistence` (`import { logger } from "@elizaos/core"`). Two layered problems:

1. **Vite's dependency optimizer ignores `resolve.alias`.** It resolves bare `@elizaos/core` via `package.json` `exports` → the **Node** build (`src/index.node.ts`), not the browser entry. (A `resolve.alias` to `index.browser.ts` fixes on-demand transforms but not the pre-bundle pass.)
2. The Node entry pulls `features/plugin-manager`, which (a) calls `process.cwd()` at module-eval (`pluginAction = createPluginAction()`), and (b) imports `fs-extra`/`graceful-fs` that monkey-patch `fs.close` at eval. The patch throws `Cannot set property close … which has only a getter` against a read-only ESM `fs` stub (and `String(symbol)` inside Vite's `browser-external` proxy throws the misleading `Cannot convert a Symbol value to a string`).

## Already tried (clears the first two error classes, not the third)

- `@elizaos/core` → `index.browser.ts` alias (on-demand only)
- Node-builtin stub in **both** a Vite plugin and the optimizer's esbuild pass (proven `Reflect.get`-deferring proxy — fixes the Symbol-coercion class)
- Ambient `process` shim via preview `<head>` (fixes the `process.cwd` class)
- `optimizeDeps.exclude` of workspace pkgs — **regressed** `RunningAppsRow`, didn't sever fs-extra
- esbuild `onResolve` marking `@elizaos/core` external — didn't sever fs-extra (the scanner still discovers it)

`fs-extra` keeps reaching the optimizer graph through core's Node entry.

## Proposed fixes (any one)

1. **(preferred)** An `optimizeDeps.esbuildOptions` `onResolve` that maps `@elizaos/core` (and subpaths) to `index.browser.ts` **as a path** (not `external`), so the optimizer scanner crawls the browser graph (no `plugin-manager` → no `fs-extra`). Untried; the precise counter to "optimizer ignores `resolve.alias`".
2. A mutable (CJS) `fs` stub that `graceful-fs` can patch without throwing.
3. Decouple `state/persistence` from a *value* import of `@elizaos/core` — import `logger` from a lighter, browser-safe entry.

## Acceptance

The 11 stories above render in `storybook dev` (headless Playwright probe), and `bun run --cwd packages/ui lint && typecheck` stay clean.
2a:T73c,## Summary

Private (`"private": true`, not-on-npm) workspace packages are never built on a fresh clone, so their `dist/` is empty and importing them throws `ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND`. This blocks `apps/app` (and any consumer) from booting its dev server on a fresh checkout in the default install mode.

Surfaced by a downstream QA smoke pass (milady-ai/milady#2179, finding #7).

## Repro

1. Fresh clone, default `bun install` (no workspace build).
2. Start the app dev server.
3. Crash:
   ```
   Error [ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND]: Cannot find module
   '.../packages/plugin-worker-runtime/node_modules/@elizaos/plugin-remote-manifest/dist/rpc-mac.js'
   imported from .../packages/plugin-worker-runtime/src/dispatch.ts
   ```

## Root cause

- `@elizaos/plugin-remote-manifest` is `"private": true` (not on npm). Its `package.json` exports `"./rpc-mac" → ./dist/rpc-mac.js`, but `dist/` is empty on a fresh clone — its build (`tsc -p tsconfig.build.json`) is never triggered by any install script.
- Its own workspace dep `@elizaos/security` is similarly unbuilt — there is a **chain** of unbuilt private workspace packages.
- Import chain: `packages/plugin-worker-runtime/src/dispatch.ts` → `@elizaos/plugin-remote-manifest/rpc-mac`.
- Note: `rpc-mac` is HMAC (message authentication), **not** macOS — the issue is platform-universal (Mac/Linux/Windows all hit it). Running local/workspace setup once builds the packages and the `dist/` persists, which is why it presents as a fresh-clone setup gap.

## Proposed fixes

- Add a dependency-ordered workspace-build step to install/postinstall (`@elizaos/security` → `@elizaos/plugin-remote-manifest` → consumers).
- Or have consumers import `src/*.ts` directly via the tsx loader (works in dev; verify production bundling).

## Severity

High — blocks fresh-clone app dev on every platform.

2e:T1679,## Summary

Decomposes the ~7.5K-LOC `plugin-lifeops` monolith into a slim orchestrator (`plugin-personal-assistant`) plus 6 new focused plugins, with the `/lifeops` dashboard view removed in favor of per-domain plugin views. No backwards-compat shims — `@elizaos/plugin-lifeops` is gone, not aliased.

## What changed

- **plugin-relationships** — entity/relationship graph (`ENTITY` action + Drizzle `app_relationships` schema). Absorbs lifeops `entities/`, `relationships/`, voice-observer bridge.

- **plugin-phone** — `+ VOICE_CALL` (Twilio) action.

- `plugin-lifeops` → `plugin-personal-assistant` (727+50 file renames, 299 cross-workspace updates, zero remaining `@elizaos/plugin-lifeops` references).

- `/lifeops` view deleted (LifeOpsPageView, LifeOpsTuiView, XR variant, `lifeops.overview` widget). PA no longer ships a view — domain plugins do.
- `.github/workflows/lifeops-bench*.yml` filenames kept (benchmark-suite identifier, not the plugin name) — required-check refs would break.

### Core foundations (Phase 3, structural only)
Scaffold-only future homes; code still lives in `plugin-personal-assistant` until the next migration pass:
- `packages/core/src/owner-state/` — OwnerFactsStore, HandoffStore, GlobalPauseStore, FirstRunService, PendingPromptsStore interfaces.
- `packages/core/src/registries/` — Anchor, Gate, EventKind, EscalationLadder, Family, Blocker registry interfaces.
- `packages/core/src/scheduled-task/` — ScheduledTask types extending core's existing task-scheduler with gates/escalation/completionCheck/anchors.
- `packages/app-core/src/dispatch/` — ConnectorRegistry, ChannelRegistry, ApprovalQueue, send-policy interfaces.
Each module has a README with `TODO(migrate:)` pointing back to the still-canonical source.

### What stayed in `plugin-personal-assistant`
The cross-domain orchestration role:
- `BRIEF`, `PRIORITIZE`, `PERSONAL_ASSISTANT`, `SCHEDULED_TASKS`, `RESOLVE_REQUEST` actions.
- Default-pack composition (daily-rhythm, morning-brief, followup-starter, habit-starters, executive-assistant).
- The entity/relationship store, scheduled-task runner, owner-state, registries, connector/channel registries — all still here, will migrate into core/app-core in follow-up passes once the new homes exist.

## Test plan

- [x] `bun run lint` — exit 0
- [x] `bun run typecheck` — exit 0 workspace-wide
- [x] `bun run build` — exit 0 workspace-wide
- [ ] Manual smoke: load PA + the new domain plugins in dev-ui, confirm each registered view renders
- [ ] Manual smoke: `BRIEF` action still composes calendar + inbox + todos + finances correctly
- [ ] DB migration plan: new plugins introduce `app_relationships`, `app_inbox`, `app_goals`, `app_blocker`, `app_finances` schemas — verify their tables co-exist with PA's existing `app_lifeops` schema during the cut-over window

## Follow-up

This PR establishes the structural decomposition. The remaining migration moves the implementations out of `plugin-personal-assistant` into the new homes:
1. Move EntityStore + RelationshipStore + merge engine → plugin-relationships.
2. Move INBOX action body + TriageRepository → plugin-inbox.
3. Move OWNER_GOALS/ROUTINES/REMINDERS/ALARMS bodies + CheckinService + default-pack fragments → plugin-goals.
4. Move BLOCK + website-blocker/ + app-blocker/ → plugin-blocker.
5. Move OWNER_FINANCES body → plugin-finances.
6. Move CALENDAR/CONFLICT_DETECT bodies + calendar-gate + travel-time → plugin-calendar.
7. Move OWNER_DOCUMENTS body → plugin-documents.
8. Move OWNER_HEALTH/SCREENTIME bodies + ActivityTracker + PresenceSignalBridge → plugin-health.
9. Move ScheduledTaskRunner + Anchor/Gate/EventKind/EscalationLadder registries + OwnerState services → @elizaos/core.
10. Move ConnectorRegistry + ChannelRegistry + ApprovalQueue + send-policy → @elizaos/app-core.
11. After (1)-(10), PA becomes ~10% of its current size — pure BRIEF/PRIORITIZE/PA orchestration + default-pack composition.

<h3>Greptile Summary</h3>

This PR introduces an **agent-surface framework** that makes every plugin view agent-controllable (registry, overlay, `useAgentElement` hook, and capability dispatch), plus a **view-action affinity layer** that upweights view-scoped runtime actions in the planner's prompt while the user is looking at that view. It also ships **sleep/wake lifecycle endpoints** for deep cold-storage of agent compute, **incremental diff backups** for agent sandboxes, and a **scheduled-backup cron** to keep agents continuously snapshotted.

- Agent-surface: `ViewAgentRegistry`, `AgentSurfaceProvider`, `useAgentElement`, `AgentElementOverlay`, and capability handlers wired into both `DynamicViewLoader` and the new `ShellViewAgentSurface` wrapper.
- Incremental backups: `agent-backup-diff.ts` computes field-level deltas; migration 0136 adds `backup_kind`/`parent_backup_id`/`content_hash` safely.
- Sleep/wake: `executeSleep` backs up then frees compute; `executeWake` re-provisions and restores.

<h3>Confidence Score: 4/5</h3>

Safe to merge; the most consequential new paths are well-tested and the DB change is backwards-compatible.

The three main feature areas all land with test coverage and the migration is safe. The pre-shutdown backup inconsistency leaves content_hash null on the most important backup rows, but is functionally safe since the column is nullable.

packages/cloud-shared/src/lib/services/eliza-sandbox.ts — the executeSleep pre-shutdown backup path

35:Td06,## Summary

Extracts the **entire** calendar surface out of `@elizaos/plugin-lifeops` into a new first-class **`@elizaos/plugin-calendar`**, keeps LifeOps fully working via thin wrappers + delegation, and wires the Odysseus `CalendarView` to the canonical calendar API (resolves #8203).

## What now lives in `@elizaos/plugin-calendar`
- **`CalendarService`** (former 1209-line mixin) + **`CalendarRepository`** + **drizzle schema** — same `app_lifeops.life_calendar_*` tables (no data migration).
- **Apple bridge**, **Google delegates**, **normalize helpers**.
- **Client methods** (`api/client-calendar.ts`, side-effect augmentation of the `@elizaos/ui` `ElizaClient`).
- **HTTP routes** — `handleCalendarRoutes` in `routes/calendar-routes.ts` (paths unchanged: `/api/lifeops/calendar/*`).
- **CALENDAR action + ~4280-line LLM handler** — `createCalendarActionRunner(deps)` + `extractCalendarPlanWithLlm`.
- **Dashboard UI** — `CalendarSection`, `EventEditorDrawer`, `useCalendarWeek` (exported via a server-graph-free `./ui` barrel).
- **Contract types** in `@elizaos/shared/contracts/calendar` (the only layer both `@elizaos/ui` and the plugins can depend on without a cycle).

## The seam (no dependency cycle — strictly `lifeops → calendar`)
Cross-domain concerns calendar can't own are **injected** from thin LifeOps wrappers (mirrors the documented plugin-health owner-action pattern):
- `CalendarHostGate` — Google connector grants + reminder-plan/audit persistence (default gate resolves Google directly + no-ops reminders; LifeOps injects the real impl at init).
- `CalendarRouteDeps` — LifeOps' HTTP plumbing (telemetry/schema-bootstrap, rate limiting, body parsing).
- `CalendarActionDeps` — trajectory LLM model runners, recent-conversation context, optional travel-time buffer.
- `CalendarSectionProps` — dashboard-shell deps (chat launcher, selection, event priming).

LifeOps retains only thin wrappers (delegation shim for the service methods, gate impl, the `CALENDAR` owner-action wrapper with `hasLifeOpsAccess` gating, the `LifeOpsCalendarSection` wrapper) — plus the legitimate cross-domain consumers (reminders/google reading the calendar cache).

## Odysseus (#8203)
`CalendarView` overlays a **read-only provider feed** (Google/Apple via the calendar client) on top of the existing local-first calendars + `.ics` (the **augment** option). Stale header fixed.

## Verification (all green)
- `@elizaos/plugin-calendar`: typecheck (0 errors) + build + **31 tests** (Apple bridge, normalize/merge units, real-PGlite `CalendarService` CRUD asserting persistence, feed aggregation, next-event context, and gate reminder-plan side effects).
- `@elizaos/plugin-lifeops`: full build (js + **views bundle** + types) + 53 calendar-touching unit tests.
- `@elizaos/plugin-task-coordinator`: build + 25 tests. `@elizaos/shared`: 777 tests + typecheck. `@elizaos/ui`: typecheck.
- No `@elizaos/plugin-lifeops` import anywhere in `plugin-calendar` (cycle-free).

## Follow-up (noted, low-harm)
A few calendar-specific formatters/delegates were copied into plugin-calendar and are now unused inside LifeOps' shared `google/format-helpers.ts` / `google-plugin-delegates.ts`; surgical removal from those multi-export files was deferred.

<details><summary><h3>Greptile Summary</h3></summary>

This PR adds end-to-end support for custom Docker image provisioning through the Eliza cloud API: the `dockerImage` field is now accepted on agent creation, validated with a regex schema, persisted to the database, and surfaced in list and detail API responses. The dashboard is wired to pass the persisted image through sandbox creation, and new E2E tests cover both the API path and the dashboard UI flow.

- **API layer** (`route.ts` for both agents and `[agentId]`): `dockerImage` is validated via a zod schema with a character-class regex, stored on create, and returned in list/detail DTOs — including the fix in `Page.tsx` that was previously hardcoding `docker_image: null`.
- **E2E stack hardening** (`stack.ts`): migrations now run as an explicit logged step before the API server starts, `DATABASE_URL` is set earlier in the process, and the stack switches to `cloud-api-e2e-server.mjs`.
- **Frontend fixes** (`agent-card.tsx`, `glass-composer.tsx`, `sandbox-proxy/page.tsx`, `vite.config.ts`): accessibility improvements (aria-label, aria-pressed), an inlined Vite alias for es-toolkit/compat shims, and a switch from `process.env.NODE_ENV` to `import.meta.env.PROD` for the sandbox proxy guard.
</details>


<h3>Confidence Score: 5/5</h3>

Safe to merge — the core dockerImage feature is properly validated, typed, and covered by new E2E tests for both the API and dashboard paths.

The change is well-scoped: the new dockerImage field is validated at the API boundary with a zod regex schema, threaded through the service layer, and verified end-to-end in both provision and dashboard tests. No existing auth or billing gates are bypassed. The ancillary changes (stack hardening, frontend shims, accessibility fixes) are self-contained improvements.

No files require special attention. The telemetry guard mismatch in main.tsx is a minor inconsistency with no observable runtime impact.

<details><summary><h3>Sequence Diagram</h3></summary>

```mermaid
sequenceDiagram
    participant Client
    participant CloudAPI as cloud-api (POST /agents)
    participant SandboxSvc as elizaSandboxService
    participant DB as agent_sandboxes
    participant ProvisionAPI as POST /agents/:id/provision
    participant ControlPlane as Control Plane

    Client->>CloudAPI: "POST /api/v1/eliza/agents {agentName, dockerImage?}"
    CloudAPI->>CloudAPI: dockerImageSchema.safeParse()
    CloudAPI->>SandboxSvc: "createAgent({agentName, dockerImage})"
    SandboxSvc->>DB: "INSERT (docker_image = dockerImage)"
    DB-->>SandboxSvc: agent row
    SandboxSvc-->>CloudAPI: agent
    CloudAPI-->>Client: "201 {id, dockerImage}"

    Client->>ProvisionAPI: POST /api/v1/eliza/agents/:id/provision
    ProvisionAPI->>DB: read docker_image
    ProvisionAPI->>ControlPlane: schedule provisioning job (image)
    ControlPlane-->>ProvisionAPI: 202 accepted
    ProvisionAPI-->>Client: "202 {agentId}"

    Note over Client,ControlPlane: poll GET /agents/:id until status=running
```
</details>

<details><summary><h3>Comments Outside Diff (1)</h3></summary>

1. `.github/workflows/coverage-gate.yml`, line 10 ([link](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/blob/cb8cb4c0614d1f25b9cddf8a24bc2cef9237a3f0/.github/workflows/coverage-gate.yml#L10)) 

   <a href="#"><img alt="P1" src="https://greptile-static-assets.s3.amazonaws.com/badges/p1.svg?v=9" align="top"></a> **Lockfile integrity bypassed on failure**

   The `|| bun install --no-frozen-lockfile` fallback means that whenever the lockfile is out of sync with `package.json` — the exact condition `--frozen-lockfile` is supposed to catch — CI silently falls through to an unconstrained install. Packages resolved during that fallback install may differ from what the lockfile records, making the build non-reproducible and removing the supply-chain integrity guarantee the frozen flag provides. If the frozen install is failing in CI today, the correct fix is to regenerate the lockfile and commit it, not to bypass the check on every future run.

<!-- /greptile_comment -->37:T3172,## Summary
- carry the upstream stale-test alignment for the current startup shell and registry/browser-route tests
- hydrate full `/api/status` once when launch/boot progress reports ready, so ChatView receives the runtime model before unlocking the composer
- align the assistant-home ui-smoke flow with the current chat workspace, typed send, scripted browser STT, push-to-talk, and desktop pill suppression
- keep the inline chat composer in its push-to-talk release state after the transcript fills the draft, so release can still submit the held turn
- update reset smoke coverage to assert the current bootstrap-token first-run form after Reset Everything
- align the scenario-runner PR coverage contract with the current assistant-home spec

## Root Cause
The stacked app-smoke branch fixed the dev chat mount, but broader Zero-Key/Scenario runs still hit stale assertions and then exposed a startup-state gap: ready launch/boot progress mapped `model` to `undefined`, which ChatView interpreted as no configured provider. The assistant-home spec also expected old home-only selectors and embedded-pill behavior that no longer exists in the current chat shell. While replacing those stale selectors, the full browser smoke exposed a real push-to-talk regression: the inline composer swapped from the release mic to the send button once STT text filled the draft, so pointer release could no longer submit the held voice turn.

39:T13ed,## Summary

This draft branch integrates the orchestrator/operator-inspector work into the elizaOS app and agent surfaces so it can be reviewed, tested, and selectively harvested. It adds richer orchestrator task APIs, operator recovery controls, edited-plan restart flow, timeline/message/usage plumbing, typed UI client coverage, and a workbench-style UI for inspecting tasks and sub-agent sessions.

This is intentionally a **draft**. The branch proves the end-to-end contracts and test coverage, but the UI should be reviewed critically before anything is treated as final visual/product direction.

## What changed

- Extended the orchestrator API surface for task detail, task lists, messages, events, timeline, usage, plan revisions, pause/resume, archive/reopen, fork, validate, retry, rerun, restart, and restart-with-edited-plan flows.
- Hardened orchestrator task service/store/mapper behavior around richer operator state, including sessions, artifacts, plan revisions, usage, active task/session counts, recovery state, and persisted task metadata.
- Expanded `OrchestratorWorkbench` with an operator inspector, task rail, sub-agent session cards, message/timeline rendering, plan/acceptance panels, recovery controls, plan editor, and message composer wiring.
- Added typed client methods and contract coverage for the new orchestrator API shapes in `packages/ui`.
- Stabilized app-shell route loading and smoke/live-stack behavior for `/`, `/chat`, `/orchestrator`, `/apps/tasks`, app routes, and settings/connectors paths.
- Added an env-gated local demo fixture mode for the smoke API stub: `ELIZA_UI_SMOKE_DEMO_ORCHESTRATOR=1`. This lets reviewers open the normal app locally and see a populated orchestrator task with demo sub-agents without needing real provider credentials.
- Added detailed and simple HTML docs describing the branch, status, vision, validation, and how the orchestrator/operator inspector fits into elizaOS.

## Reviewer note for Shaw / agent review

The useful review path is probably:

1. Start with `plugins/plugin-agent-orchestrator/src/api/orchestrator-routes.ts` and the matching service/store changes to verify the backend contract and recovery semantics.
2. Check `packages/ui/src/api/client-agent.ts` and `client-types-cloud.ts` to confirm the frontend contract matches the route behavior.
3. Review `plugins/plugin-task-coordinator/src/OrchestratorWorkbench.tsx` for product/UX direction, but treat this part as rough draft UI rather than final polish.
4. Review the smoke fixture in `packages/app-core/scripts/playwright-ui-smoke-api-stub.mjs` as local test/demo support only. It is behind `ELIZA_UI_SMOKE_DEMO_ORCHESTRATOR=1` and should not affect default smoke behavior.
5. Use the tests below to decide which parts are ready to keep versus split or refine.

## Commits
1. **auto-spawn opencode+cerebras on first message** — messaging a task with no live agent now spawns one (was the unimplemented `elizaos` default → `spawn ENOENT`); default framework → vendored opencode; per-task workdir.
2. **Claude-Code-style streaming workbench view** — coalesces streamed stdout chunks into one markdown turn; renders `tool_running` events as structured tool-call cards; typed status notices; finished-session tools resolve to ✓; fixes cross-task transcript contamination.
3. **key view-manager cards by viewType+id** — removes 9 duplicate-React-key console errors.
4. **real interleaved diff viewer** — LCS line-diff with old/new gutters + +/-/context coloring (replaces the flat all-removals-then-all-additions); unit-tested.
5. **Stop / Esc interrupt** — a prominent "Agent working… / Stop (Esc)" bar; global Esc cancels the run (proven: stopped a run in ~0.5s, work halted 5/12 files).
6. **test fixes** — update two unit tests to the auto-spawn + opencode-default behavior.
7. **live SSE transport** — per-task change bus + `GET /tasks/:taskId/stream` SSE endpoint + an EventSource client; the room updates live (sub-second) instead of polling, polling kept only as reconnect fallback.

<h3>Greptile Summary</h3>

This PR wires up a Claude-Code/Codex-style live coding workbench on top of the orchestrator plugin: SSE push transport replaces polling, streamed agent chunks are coalesced into coherent turns, tool calls render as structured diff/shell cards, a stop/Esc interrupt bar halts running agents, and the LCS-based `lineDiff` produces real interleaved diffs instead of flat remove-then-add blocks.

- **SSE transport** (`orchestrator-routes.ts`, `client-agent.ts`): per-task pub/sub bus with `subscribeTaskChanges`; heartbeat keeps proxies alive; debounced client-side refetch on each `change` ping; polling retained as reconnect fallback.
- **Streaming workbench** (`orchestrator-stream.tsx`): `buildConversation` merges same-lane agent chunks, collapses multi-event tool calls by ID, and resolves perpetual spinners for finished sessions; `MarkdownText` renders inline code/bold/links and fenced code blocks.
- **Auto-spawn** (`orchestrator-task-service.ts`): messaging a task with no live agent now spawns an opencode+Cerebras session automatically; the vendored opencode backend replaces the unimplemented elizaos default.

<h3>Confidence Score: 4/5</h3>

Safe to merge with one fix: the EventSource client needs an onerror handler to prevent infinite reconnect loops when the task is deleted or returns a non-2xx response.

The SSE client in `streamOrchestratorTask` has no `onerror` handler. When the server returns a 404 (task not found or deleted) or any other error, the browser's EventSource automatically retries every ~3 seconds indefinitely, hammering the endpoint until the component unmounts. The polling fallback is already in place, so simply closing the EventSource on error loses no functionality. Everything else — the conversation builder, diff renderer, stop/Esc bar, cross-task contamination fix, and auto-spawn path — looks correct and well-guarded.

packages/ui/src/api/client-agent.ts — the `streamOrchestratorTask` function needs an `onerror` handler that calls `source.close()`.

<h3>Sequence Diagram</h3>

```mermaid
sequenceDiagram
    participant Browser as Browser (OrchestratorWorkbench)
    participant API as orchestrator-routes (SSE)
    participant Service as OrchestratorTaskService
    participant Agent as opencode Agent

    Browser->>API: GET /tasks/:taskId/stream (EventSource)
    API->>Service: subscribeTaskChanges(taskId, listener)
    API-->>Browser: "data: {type:"ready"}"

    loop Agent working
        Agent->>Service: session event / tool_running / usage
        Service->>Service: emitChange(taskId)
        Service->>API: listener() fires
        API-->>Browser: "data: {type:"change"}"
        Browser->>Browser: scheduleRefetch (debounce 150ms)
        Browser->>API: GET /tasks/:taskId/messages + events
        API-->>Browser: updated conversation tail
        Browser->>Browser: buildConversation → render
    end

    Browser->>API: Stop (Esc / button)
    API->>Service: stopOrchestratorAgent(taskId, sessionId)
    Service->>Agent: terminate

    Browser->>Browser: component unmount / task switch
    Browser->>API: EventSource.close()
    API->>API: req close → cleanup (clearInterval + unsubscribe + res.end)
```

<!-- /greptile_comment -->3b:T139f,## Problem
1. A false `state_lost` probe plus unbounded respawn could cascade duplicate sub-agents.
2. Asked "what did you change / show the diff", the parent confabulated edits because completion narration wasn't grounded in the actual change set.

## Change
- Bound respawn and fix the `state_lost` false-positive (stops the cascade).
- Capture the real change set from git (`name-status` vs the spawn baseline) unioned with the agent's edit/write tool-call paths (covers gitignored deploy targets), excluding files already dirty at spawn.
- Surface it via a provider gated by a new general `Provider.alwaysInResponseState` flag (reaches the simple-reply path without core naming the plugin), with a staleness guard that won't surface an older change set once a newer task has run.
- Dispatch the coding sub-agent when the planner names a simile of the TASKS action.

## Testing
orchestrator suite green (393); `workspace-diff`/`coding-session-changes`/`sub-agent-router`/`acp-service` unit tests green; core message-routing/stage1/trajectory green.

<!-- greptile_comment -->

<h3>Greptile Summary</h3>

This PR fixes two interconnected orchestrator bugs: a false-positive `state_lost` health-check probe (probing `.stream.ndjson` which was never written for native sessions, flipping every idle `ready` session to `errored` and triggering unbounded `session_state_lost` respawn cascades), and completion narrations that confabulated edits because the parent read the sub-agent's raw tool transcript instead of the real change set.

- **Health-check gate**: `runHealthCheck` now skips any session not in `ACP_MIDFLIGHT_SESSION_STATUSES` (`running`/`busy`/`tool_running`), fixing the false-positive cascade trigger; the probe target is also corrected from `.stream.ndjson` to the actual `.json` artifact.
- **Respawn backstop**: `SubAgentRouter` now tracks `session_state_lost` events per stable origin lineage (`taskRoomId + agentType`) and caps cross-session respawn at `DEFAULT_STATE_LOST_RESPAWN_CAP = 3`, resetting on a genuine `task_complete`.
- **Ground-truth change set**: At `task_complete`, `workspace-diff.ts` captures git `--name-status` output plus agent tool-call paths, persisted to session metadata and surfaced via a new `CODING_SESSION_CHANGES` provider flagged with `alwaysInResponseState: true`.

<h3>Confidence Score: 4/5</h3>

<!-- /greptile_comment -->3c:T23fa,## Summary
Wire up a real fleet rollout for cloud agents. When the registry-side digest of the configured agent tag moves, the daemon now enqueues a blue/green \`agent_upgrade\` job per running agent still on the old digest, capped at 3 in flight, and executes them as zero-downtime cross-node swaps.

Today's reality: a new image only reaches the fleet when an agent crashes or someone restarts it manually, so the cluster runs a mix of versions until everyone naturally cycles. The morning of 2026-05-29 had agents on \`develop\` alongside agents on \`eliza-tla:patched\` for that reason.

## How it works
- **Probe**: the reconciler calls \`resolveImageDigest(containersEnv.defaultAgentImage())\` against ghcr.io every 30s. Cached 60s. Null on bare image names, non-ghcr registries, or registry errors — those cycles are no-ops, the agent stays put.
- **Pick a target node**: \`getAvailableNode({ excludeNodeId: currentNode })\`. Different node guarantees no container-name collision (names are deterministic \`agent-\${id}\` per docker daemon). Autoscaler-on-demand path fires automatically if no other node has free capacity.
- **Provision blue + health check**: regular provisioner path with the new image. If it never goes healthy, we tear down blue and bail — the agent keeps running on old with old image. Auto-rollback.
- **Atomic swap**: one UPDATE on \`agent_sandboxes\` (bridge_url, health_url, node_id, container_name, ports, docker_image, image_digest). New HTTP requests hit blue from this point on.
- **Drain + remove old**: \`docker stop -t 30\` SIGTERM (30s drain for in-flight HTTP + websockets), \`docker rm -f\`. Allocated count decremented on the old node.
- **Cleanup**: the autoscaler's existing idle-node drain handles nodes that empty out as agents rebalance.

## Schema
New \`image_digest text\` column on \`agent_sandboxes\`, stamped at provision time. NULL on rows that predate this PR is treated as "differs from target" — first rollout sweeps the existing fleet onto the current digest, after which the registry-vs-stored compare is exact.

Migration 0135, additive, no backfill required.

## Test plan
- [x] 17 unit tests on the registry probe — parsing edge cases (port-in-registry, bare image names, empty tag, malformed), 404 / 5xx / network-error handling, only-ghcr policy, positive + negative caching with TTL expiry verification.
- [x] Smoke test on the job-type registry — \`AGENT_UPGRADE\` is registered and unique.
- [x] \`biome check\` clean on all changed files.
- [x] \`typecheck\` clean for the changed files.
- [ ] Manual end-to-end on staging: push a fresh \`:develop\`, watch the reconciler enqueue + execute 3 swaps per cycle, verify blue boots healthy, swap is atomic, old container gets a SIGTERM drain.

## Out of scope (follow-ups)
- Per-agent opt-out flag (some agents may need to be pinned to a digest).
- Idle-aware scheduling (skip swap when an agent has an active websocket).
- Webhook from \`build-agent-image.yml\` to trigger an immediate rollout rather than waiting for the 30s reconciler tick.

<h3>Greptile Summary</h3>

This PR introduces a fleet-upgrade reconciler that detects when the ghcr.io digest of the configured agent tag has moved and performs rolling blue/green swaps across the fleet, capped at 3 concurrent upgrades. It adds a `registry-probe` module for cached digest resolution, a new `AGENT_UPGRADE` job type with full enqueue/execute machinery, `image_digest` stamping on `agent_sandboxes`, and cross-node placement via `excludeNodeId`.

- **`registry-probe.ts`**: 60 s cached HEAD-based digest lookup against ghcr.io only; returns `null` for non-ghcr refs and all error paths; 17 unit tests cover parsing edge cases, HTTP failure modes, cache TTL, and negative caching.
- **`executeUpgrade` in `eliza-sandbox.ts`**: provisions a blue container on a different node, health-checks it, atomically swaps the DB row, then best-effort SIGTERMs the old container; auto-rollback if blue doesn't come up healthy.
- **`processFleetUpgradeCycle` in `provisioning-worker.ts`**: per-tick reconciler that probes the registry, counts in-flight jobs, queries candidates via `IS DISTINCT FROM`, and enqueues up to the remaining capacity slack.

<h3>Confidence Score: 4/5</h3>

Safe to merge with one fix: concurrent stop/suspend/delete can race with an in-flight upgrade and leave an orphaned running container

`hasActiveProvisionJobTx` only queries for `AGENT_PROVISION` jobs, so user-triggered stop, suspend, or delete operations are not blocked while a 60-90 s blue provisioning is in progress. If a stop lands first, the upgrade's unconditional swap UPDATE writes the blue container's coordinates into the stopped row, leaving a running container that receives no traffic and has no automatic cleanup path unless the idle-node autoscaler eventually drains that node. Everything else is solid.

packages/cloud-shared/src/lib/services/eliza-sandbox.ts — specifically `hasActiveProvisionJobTx` and the atomic swap UPDATE in `executeUpgrade`

1. `packages/cloud-shared/src/lib/services/docker-sandbox-provider.ts`, line 627-632 ([link](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/blob/bba2d0185138bef187618b8309291075b77904cc/packages/cloud-shared/src/lib/services/docker-sandbox-provider.ts#L627-L632)) 

   <a href="#"><img alt="P2" src="https://greptile-static-assets.s3.amazonaws.com/badges/p2.svg?v=9" align="top"></a> **Overcommit risk when old-node SSH is unreachable**

   `decrementAllocated` runs unconditionally after the stop/rm attempts, even if both fail (e.g., a network partition to the old node). If the SSH calls throw and the container stays alive, `allocated_count` on the old node is decremented past its real container count. Subsequent scheduling decisions will treat that node as having more free capacity than it actually has, and the next agent provisioned there can exceed the node's actual limits. The autoscaler's idle-drain will eventually reconcile, but the gap can span multiple reconciler cycles on a busy fleet.
</details>
